# T2 LEDGER — the 3D dish viewer & the dish page (P00501–P01000)

Terminal 2 of 30, sweep #6. Branch `sweep6/t2-guest-3d-item`, worktree
`/Users/aevinite/Documents/Projects/sweep6/T2`, dev server port **4102**.

**Territory (write):** `app/item/**` · `app/r/[restaurant]/item/**` · `app/view/**` ·
`components/PublicModelViewer.tsx` · `components/ModelToastHost.tsx` ·
`components/InfinityLoader.tsx` · `components/Particles.tsx` · `components/StarRating.tsx` ·
`lib/modelLoader.ts`

## The budget these 500 phases were written to

| slice | share | phases | what it means here |
|---|---|---|---|
| reading the code for correctness | ~40% | 200 | P00501–P00565, P00566–P00610, P00611–P00700, P00726–P00800 (the bulk of every file block) |
| conformance to the project's own written rules | ~20% | 100 | CLAUDE.md 11-point module checklist, egress rules, back-button manager, tap-guard, offline, white-label, REJECTED-IDEAS |
| watching it run headless on port 4102 | ~15% | 75 | Playwright against my own port, real dish opens, real 3D opens |
| screenshots I actually Read | ~10% | 50 | desktop 1280×800 **and** Samsung A35 360×780 dpr3, both skins |
| tracing one change across panels | ~10% | 50 | menu → dish → 3D → cart → toast → back, and the two tenant doors |
| my own judgment about a real restaurant | ~5% | 25 | is this delightful on a mid-range phone on weak wifi, or is it a wait |

**Per-file floor:** every file I own has at least one phase; the files named in my scope
(`lib/modelLoader.ts`, `components/PublicModelViewer.tsx`, the item pages, the view route) have
ten or more.

**Not a finding here** (pre-empted by the prompt, re-checked against `docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md`):
Draco compression (done) · the heavy tier deliberately not preloaded by `MenuView` (2026-06-25) ·
R28 no ceiling on how many models the menu preloads · R25 legend · dark-by-default.

## FOURTH PASS — SWEEP #7, 2026-08-22, against `origin/main` b64951ad (worktree `wt-s7-t2`, port **4202**)

**Every one of the 500 rows below was re-run and its `result` updated in place.** 331 by static
assertion against the files as they are today; the rest driven headless on my own port. Nothing was
taken on trust from sweep #6 — where a row said "measured", it was measured again.

| | |
|---|---|
| rows re-run | **500 / 500** |
| ✅ | **488** |
| ⏭ | **11** — each with its reason re-checked this run, not copied forward |
| ❌ | **1** — P00852, an open handoff into another territory's file |
| **REGRESSIONS found** | **1 — P00649**, fixed in this branch as item 1 |
| rows moved OFF ⏭ | **4** — P00679, P00704, P00863, P00864 |

**The regression, first, because it is the point of this whole exercise.** `P00649` asserts that no
orphaned animation frame survives leaving the 3D screen. It was ✅ in sweep #6. It is a real, ongoing
fault today: measured on the DISH page, twenty seconds after leaving the 3D screen, **six connector-line
loops still running at 360 animation frames a second, forever**. Sweep #6 missed it because it drove one
dish→3D→back cycle and waited long enough for the loop to start while the screen was still mounted; the
window that leaks is leaving **1–3.5 s after the model paints**, which is what a diner who glances and
taps Back actually does. Cause and fix: commit `item 1`.

**Four rows stopped being skipped.** Sweep #6 could not drive the non-#1 white-label path on `/view`
because "no restaurant other than French House has a dish with a 3D model". That reason was too strong:
the accent, the dish name, the price and the Back link are all resolved from `?r=` **before** any model
work, so `/view/Croissant?from=salmon-nigiri&r=sakura-sushi` drives the whole white-label path on a real
second tenant. It does: `--accent #db2777`, "Salmon Nigiri", ₹249, Back into Sakura, tab title
"3D View — Sakura Sushi". Only the photograph of a non-#1 *model* is still impossible here.

**Two things changed underneath this territory since the last pass, and neither is a code fault.**
French House now has the **ratings switch OFF**, so its entire review area correctly renders nothing —
the star-picker rows were re-run on sakura-sushi instead of flipping a shared switch. And
`app/globals.css` moved by 77 lines under other terminals; every class this territory depends on was
re-grepped against today's file and all are present.

**One measurement trap worth passing on:** a hit-test on a dev server can be blocked by
`nextjs-portal`, Next's own development overlay, which never ships. Two of seventeen points across the
3D Add button "failed" for that reason alone. Discount it before believing a coverage finding.

## Third pass — re-run against the DEPLOYED backup site (2026-08-18)

Asked for by the owner after item 14 went in: *"do 500 phase test again on backup site to check
anything not broken and everything change working fine"*. So this pass ran against
**https://3-d-backup.vercel.app**, not localhost — and against `7b9ce218`, which is the deployed
commit and also carries the other terminals' merged work, so it checks that nobody broke anything
of mine and that mine broke nothing of theirs.

| what | result |
|---|---|
| runtime phases driven on the live site (2 batches) | **52 checks, 52 green** |
| `verify:cache --base https://3-d-backup.vercel.app` | **PASS** — download-once holds on the real site |
| `verify:slow-load --base https://3-d-backup.vercel.app` | **PASS** — BACK reachable, dish + ₹550 readable while waiting, overlay at 15.5s, ticket names the right dish |
| `verify:3d-viewer` (35 checks) · `verify:static` (all 30 guards) · `verify:taps` (33) · `verify:rejected` · `verify:dead-css` · `verify:loadall` · `verify:ui` | all **PASS** on the deployed commit |
| `npm run typecheck` | clean |
| `npm run lint` | 0 errors (484 warnings — up from 444 because of other terminals' merged code, none of it mine) |

**Every fix confirmed on the live site, not just merged:**

| fix | live evidence |
|---|---|
| F1 BACK works while the model loads | loading panel `z-index: 20`; hit-test on BACK returns the button; a real tap left the screen |
| F2 the table gate on the 3D Add | source-guarded; the join overlay opens instead of a silent add |
| F3 the dish is readable while waiting | bar on screen with "Avocado & Cream Cheese" and ₹550, Add enabled and reachable |
| F4 the ticket names the right dish | "✦ Avocado & Cream Cheese in 3D · ready to view" |
| F5 the engine file arrives once | exactly **one** 200 response for model-viewer.min.js |
| F6/F7 maintenance + link preview | source-guarded on both doors; both answer 404 for a missing dish |
| F8 + item 14 the tab knows its restaurant | cold `?r=sakura-sushi` → pin set before React; widgets asked about `…0005`, dish ₹320 (vs #1's ₹550) |
| I4 Add owns its edge | **0 of 152** points across the button belong to anything else; row `z-index: 50` |
| I5 the floating bar is phone/tablet only | absent at 1280, present at 360 |
| the pin cannot be injected through | hostile `?r=` pins nothing, injects no element, fires no dialog |

**One row was CORRECTED rather than "fixed":** P00654 asserted the `lfh:*` listener count returns to
zero. It does not, and should not — `ModelToastHost` is mounted once in `components/GuestChrome.tsx`
for the life of the tab. Measured over three full dish→3D→back cycles the count stayed at exactly 1,
i.e. **constant, not growing**, which is what "no leak" actually means. The row's wording is fixed
above so the next sweep runs the right check instead of chasing a non-problem. (`lfh:currency-changed`
likewise settles and stays flat; the negative numbers a naive proxy shows are removals whose adds
happened in a previous document.)

Result key: ✅ pass · ❌ real problem (goes to `T2-findings.md`) · ⏭ skipped, with a written reason.

## Second pass — the numbers

Every row below was **executed twice**: once to find, once after the fixes, from the top.

| | pass 1 | pass 2 (this file's results) |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ | 476 | **488** |
| ❌ | 12 (findings F1–F9 + the three preserved-fix corrections) | **0** |
| ⏭ | 12 | **12** — each with a written reason and a "NEXT SWEEP:" instruction in its row |

The twelve `⏭` fall into four honest groups, none of them "I ran out of time":

1. **Would write to the shared dev database mid-sweep** (P00763, P00815, P00992, P00993) — twenty-nine
   other terminals are driving this stack. Each row says what to run instead, alone.
2. **The dev stack has no fixture for it** (P00679, P00704, P00863, P00990) — no restaurant other than
   French House has a dish with a 3D model, and Aangan's guest menu answers 404 here.
3. **Another terminal's guard cannot be run from a worktree** (P00697, P00813) — `verify:offline` is
   hard-pinned to port 4000. Raised as 🔗 HANDOFF H4.
4. **Deliberate non-actions, recorded so they are not mistaken for gaps** (P00987 `verify:everything`
   belongs to the merge terminal; P00988 the dev server, stopped at hand-off).

Nine real problems were found and **all nine are fixed** (`T2-findings.md` F1–F9). Three improvements
were built (`T2-improvements.md` I1–I3). Five 🔗 HANDOFF rows name a file outside this territory and
the exact change it needs.

| P00649 | …but `_loop()` re-arms itself every frame — prove no orphaned rAF survives navigation | headless: open /view, navigate away, assert no rAF callback keeps firing | ✅ | measured: 0 requestAnimationFrame callbacks in the 2s after leaving /view · **S7:** **S7 REGRESSION, FOUND AND FIXED (item 1).** Re-measured with four dish→3D→back cycles: SIX `_loop` chains still running at 360 rAF/s on the DISH page, 20s after leaving, forever. Sweep #6's single-cycle measurement missed it because the window is leaving 1–3.5s after the model paints. Cause: `requestRef` holds only the latest handle, and `handleLoad`'s 800ms reveal timer was never cleared, so a loop could START after unmount. Fixed with `aliveRef` + timer collection; measured after: 0 rAF/s at t+0.5s, +3s, +8s and +20s, while the loop still runs on the open viewer. | P16075 | Judgment — is the first 3D open delightful or a wait? Honest: a WAIT. Measured today, the model needs several seconds on a cold open. What makes it bearable is that from 2.5s the diner has the dish's name, its price, a working Add button and a working BACK, and from this run the line that teaches them to spin it is finally readable. Every later open in the same visit is instant — 0 model bytes across six further dish opens | reasoned, from what this run measured | ✅ | · **S8** ✓ re-run 2026-09-02 (worktree `wt-s8-t2`, port **4302**, against `origin/main` fb3477b9) — re-run against the files as they are today; unchanged and still true |
| P16076 | Judgment — is 32 seconds too long before admitting defeat? Still long, still right. The screen is useful throughout, the ticket brings the diner back if they walk away, and shortening it would turn a slow connection into a false failure | reasoned, from what this run measured | ✅ | · **S8** ✓ re-run 2026-09-02 (worktree `wt-s8-t2`, port **4302**, against `origin/main` fb3477b9) — re-run against the files as they are today; unchanged and still true |
| P16077 | Judgment — should the 'still preparing' step exist at all, or go straight to unavailable? It should exist. ## ROUND 2 — THE SEVEN DECISIONS THE OWNER PICKED · `P52495`–`P52544` (2026-09-01)

He read T2's report and answered: *"you can do eight 9 10 … for the 11th … there should be a visible
error … Maybe it's already there so yeah, just telling you if not there, you can do 12. Also, you
don't have to do 13th one … 14 ones I don't know whatever you recommend. Do that … you can do 15
once I give you permission."*

**Three of the seven turned out to be already built.** That is the most useful thing in this block:
two of them were already solved at a LOWER level than the report understood, and one had been fixed
by another terminal after the report was written. Building them would have left the product with two
mechanisms doing one job — which is exactly what `CLAUDE.md`'s "a new way replaces the old one" bans.

## THE 5-PHASE TEST OF THE WHOLE BOUNDARY — run against the LIVE backup site (2026-09-01)

His instruction after the merge: *"after that, it is live plan whole five phases test around your
boundaries, make sure it covers everything the test and find the bugs if there are any, and if there
are any fix."*

So this is not a re-run of the ledger — it is a fresh plan in five phases, designed to cover the
whole territory, and executed against **https://3-d-backup.vercel.app**, not localhost. Ids are
`L1.x`–`L5.x` (an `L` prefix, deliberately outside the `P` registry: these are a live-site sweep of
ground the `P` rows already own, not new permanent checks).

| phase | what it covered | result |
|---|---|---|
| **1 · the download-once engine** | the singleton and its whole public surface, download-once across a real tapped journey, the engine script arriving once, the byte budget and the entry guard at their limits, and a dropped signal not writing a dish off | **18 ✅ · 0 ❌** |
| **2 · the 3D screen** | the happy path top to bottom, the details sheet, the phone's Back, an unknown restaurant in the link, a nonsense folder, the tab title, a slow model through both patience overlays, a blocked engine CDN, and leaving the screen three times | **30 ✅ · 0 ❌** |
| **3 · the dish page** | first paint and every element on it, both doors agreeing, all three 404s, the photo lightbox and its gestures, prev/next at both ends of a category, the table gate, and the pinned Add bar handing over | **33 ✅ · 0 ❌** |
| **4 · the look** | two screens × two skins × three sizes, machine-checked for leaked code text, page-level sideways scroll, spill past the right edge and text-over-text, then screenshots Read; plus two non-#1 restaurants' own colours on both screens | **16 ✅ · 0 ❌** |
| **5 · the rules** | offline both ways on both screens, the back-button manager, the real egress cost of one dish open, cross-door truth, the tap guard, and the one thing left open | **18 ✅ · 0 ❌** |
| | **TOTAL** | **115 ✅ · 0 ❌** |

**Five rows came back red and all five were my own test being wrong.** Recorded because a withdrawn
finding is worth as much as a confirmed one — it stops the next run re-filing it:

| row | what it claimed | what was actually true |
|---|---|---|
| L1.5 | a model file was downloaded twice | I navigated with `page.goto()`, a FULL page load, which clears an in-memory cache by design. Re-run as a diner does it — landing on the menu and TAPPING through — every file was fetched exactly once |
| L3.7 | Add to Cart was not live | it is enabled and tappable. The live label is **"Add to order"**; my test expected the words "Add to Cart" |
| L3.29 | the table gate did not say which table | it does: *"MY LITTLE FRENCH HOUSE · Which table are you at?"* with an input, Scan QR and Continue. I sampled the first 120 characters of the page BEHIND the overlay |
| L4.7 | a hotspot card hung 32px off the right edge | it does, at ~3s — while it is at **opacity 0**, hidden by the reveal reset. Sampled every 400ms for 12s: **zero** moments where a card was off-screen AND visible. The `edge = 8` nudge lands before it fades in |
| L5.16 | AR did not refuse visibly | it does, in **400ms**. My test clicked AR and then sampled 3.5s later, after the ticket had self-dismissed |

### ⚠️ A CORRECTION TO ROUND 2, found by this phase and worth more than any green row

**"After an offline reload the client JavaScript never boots" is TRUE ON THE DEV SERVER AND FALSE ON
THE LIVE SITE.** Measured on production, offline, after one warm visit: `--lfh-offbar-h` is `69px`,
`__lfh_modelLoader` is alive, React's own offline bar renders, and the page is INTERACTIVE — tapping
the photo opened the lightbox. So the dish page was never a frozen screen for a real diner; it is
frozen only under `next dev`, whose chunks the service worker does not hold.

This is the same class of trap as this ledger's own standing pre-empt about the dev server compiling
each route on first hit. **What it changes:**

* **Item 11's static bar is INSURANCE, not the rescue I described it as.** On production React's own
  bar already appears. The static one stands down whenever React is alive (proven, and guarded), so
  it is not a duplicate — but it was sold on a fault that only exists in development.
* **Item 9 is unaffected and stands on its own merits:** no spinner, one fewer browser read, and the
  dish in the server HTML — all three verified on the live site (`L3.1`, `L5.12`, and the raw HTML).
* The honest framing of "the dish page offline" is therefore: it already worked on production, and
  item 9 makes it arrive sooner and cost less.

**The lesson for the next run: never conclude "the framework does not run" from a dev server.**
Re-check it against the deployed site before it becomes a finding.

## SWEEP #8 · T2's SECOND 500 — `P56201`–`P56700`, planned fresh and driven against the LIVE site

The owner's word, after the first block was merged and deployed on 2026-09-02: *"after that, it is
live plan whole five hundred phases test around your boundaries, make sure it covers everything the
test and find the bugs if there are any, and if there are any fix."*

So this is **not** a re-run of the block above. It is a fresh plan for the same seven files, and the
plan was made by **measuring the territory** rather than by guessing at it:

| what was counted | number |
|---|---|
| lines across the seven files | **3,442** |
| state fields (`useState`) | **43** |
| refs | **10** |
| effects | **21** |
| handlers | **31** |
| methods on the download engine | **36** |
| `if` branches | **181** |
| rendered ids | **30** |
| distinct named things in the whole territory | **260** |
| …of those, never mentioned by ANY of the 1,438 checks that already covered it | **85** |

Those 85 are what this block is built around, plus a full re-drive of both screens against
**https://3-d-backup.vercel.app** — the deployed site, not localhost — because that is where the
owner asked for it after the merge.

**Result: 500 written, 500 executed, 500 ✅ · 0 ❌ · 0 ⏭.**

Read that honestly. **Five rows came back red during the run and every one of them was my own test
being wrong**, which is recorded in the note on each rather than quietly re-run until green:

| what my test claimed | what was actually true |
|---|---|
| the review tabs were missing | French House has `ratings: false`. The area correctly returns null. Aangan, with ratings on, shows the tabs — so the same code gives both answers from the restaurant's own switch |
| 40 elements spilled past the right edge | all 40 were inside `.related-section`, which scrolls sideways **by design**. Zero outside a scroller |
| a hotspot's bullets hung off the edge | its wrapper is at opacity 0 during the reveal. Sampled 16 times over 11s: **zero** moments off-screen AND visible. Sweep #7 filed and withdrew this same claim |
| the dish page's Add button was unreachable | it starts at **y=921 on a 780px screen** — below the fold, which is the entire reason the pinned bar exists. The pinned bar's button IS reachable, and the real one is too once scrolled to |
| `setQueue` and `stopAll` were dead API | they are called from `components/MenuView.tsx`, the guest menu grid — a file this terminal does not own, so a territory-only search could not see them |

**And one row found a real hole, which is the most valuable thing in this block.** Every behaviour
this territory's guards defend was **sabotaged one at a time** — 25 of them — and the guard re-run
after each. 22 went red immediately. One was covered by a different guard (`verify:slow-load`, which
drives the screen). **Two stayed green over their own fault**, and are fixed in this run as item 9.

| block | rows | subject |
|---|---|---|
| A+B | 92 | every state field, ref and effect, named one by one |
| C+D+E | 114 | every handler, every engine method, every id, every conditional |
| F | 83 | both screens on the LIVE site, two restaurants, two skins, three sizes |
| G+H+I | 108 | 25 sabotages, the measured cost of four journeys, the project's own rules |
| J | 45 | cross-panel journeys, judgment, run discipline |
| K | 22 | the download engine driven in-page on the DEPLOYED bundle |
| L+M | 36 | the Add button re-tested properly, and what is still open |

`P56701` onwards is NOT this terminal's — its pre-allocated block ends at `P56700`, and this block
uses it exactly to the last id.

| P56648 | Judgment — is the first 3D open delightful or a wait? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Honest: still a WAIT. Measured on the live site: 11,279 KB for a dish → 3D journey, of which ~11 MB is the model and 253 KB the engine. Everything AROUND the wait is now right — the dish, its price and the Add button arrive within 2.5s, BACK works throughout, and the two patience overlays are honest. But the download itself is the product's weakest link and no amount of screen polish changes that. |
| P56649 | Judgment — …so what would actually fix it? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Smaller models, not more code. R28 rules out capping how many are preloaded, and Draco is done. The remaining lever is the export settings on the models themselves — which is a content decision, not a code one, and not this terminal's to take. |
| P56650 | Judgment — is 32 seconds too long before admitting defeat? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Still long, still right. The screen is useful for all 32 of them — dish, price, Add, BACK — and the 15s card says something true in the meantime. Shortening it would write off models that do arrive on restaurant wi-fi. |
| P56651 | Judgment — should the 'still preparing' step exist at all? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes. Measured: the model regularly lands between 15s and 32s on a throttled connection, and that card is the only thing that stops the wait reading as a broken page. |
| P56652 | Judgment — is the hint pill worth the screen space? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes, and it was worth nothing before sweep #7 — it was painted 32px underneath the dish bar, so the one sentence that teaches a diner the dish can be turned had never been readable on a phone. |
| P56653 | Judgment — is the AR button honest on a phone that cannot do AR? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes. It says so in one sentence within 400ms and points at the phone, rather than doing nothing. |
| P56654 | Judgment — does the 3D screen need a way back to the MENU as well as to the dish? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | No. BACK goes where the diner came from, which is the dish; the dish page's own Back to menu is one more tap. Two back buttons on one screen is worse. |
| P56655 | Judgment — is the details sheet the right home for allergens on the 3D screen? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes. One tap from the bar, it honours the restaurant's allergy switch, and it uses the same shared icons as the dish page so the two can never disagree. |
| P56656 | Judgment — should the 3D screen show the dish's photo anywhere? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | No. The diner arrived FROM the photo. Showing it again competes with the thing they came to see. |
| P56657 | Judgment — is the pinned Add bar a nag on a phone? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | No. Measured on the live site: the real button starts at y=921 on a 780px screen, so without the bar every diner reads the dish and then has to scroll to buy it. It carries the price, it stands down as the real button arrives, and it is gone entirely on a laptop. |
| P56658 | Judgment — is 'You might also like' worth its ten cards? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes, and it costs almost nothing — the images are lazy, the data is the light list the page already holds, and it never suggests a dish you cannot order. |
| P56659 | Judgment — should the related row ever suggest a sold-out dish? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | No, and it does not. Recommending something a diner cannot have is a dead end. |
| P56660 | Judgment — is 8 seconds the right deadline for the dish read? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes. It is the floor's own patience, and since the server hands the dish down the page now SHOWS the dish while it waits — so the deadline only ever decides when to offer Try again, not whether anything is on screen. |
| P56661 | Judgment — should the honest card say 'no internet' rather than 'cannot reach the menu'? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | No. A phone can have full bars and still not reach us. The card says the true thing and offers both a retry and a way back. |
| P56662 | Judgment — should Try again retry by itself? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Both would be better; the button is the honest minimum and it is the one a diner can act on. Left as it is. |
| P56663 | Judgment — is the review area's disappearance on a ratings-off restaurant right? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes — and it is now proven on two real tenants in the same run: French House (ratings off) shows nothing and reads no review rows; Aangan (ratings on) shows the tabs. One switch, two honest answers. |
| P56664 | Judgment — is the neutral 'Menu' title on a dead dish link the right call? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes, for now. Naming the restaurant would need a database read on a 404 page, which is a bad trade. Neutral says nothing untrue about anybody, and it removes us from a diner's browser — which was the actual fault. |
| P56665 | Judgment — are 44px buttons on the 3D screen an improvement or just bigger? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | An improvement, and I looked at it rather than trusting the number: the pills read better at radius 22 than they did at 17 on a 40px control, and the top bar grew 64px → 68px with nothing else moving. |
| P56666 | Judgment — is anything in this territory dead weight for a real restaurant? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | No. Every state field, every effect and every handler was named and checked this run; the heaviest thing on either screen is the related row and its images are lazy. |
| P56667 | Judgment — what is the single most valuable thing this run found? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Item 3 — the heart. It was a three-day-old regression that wrote a diner's saved list wrong in a way they could see on another screen, and no guard covered it. |
| P56668 | Judgment — …and the most dangerous? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Item 1 — the folder-name collision. Nobody has hit it because only one restaurant has a 3D dish, so it would have gone live on the day a second restaurant uploaded one. |
| P56669 | Judgment — …and the most uncomfortable? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Item 9 — two guards that stayed green over the thing they defend. A green suite that is not actually watching is worse than no suite, because it buys false confidence. |
| P56670 | Judgment — what would a real restaurant ask us to remove from these screens? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Nothing on the 3D screen. On the dish page, possibly the related row — but it is the only thing that moves a diner from one dish to a second. |

## SWEEP #8 · T2's THIRD 500 — `P95201`–`P95700`, aimed at what rounds 1 and 2 could not reach

The owner's word, after round 2 was merged and deployed on 2026-09-02: *"after making it live and
merging plan 500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everything within your
boundaries and test everything again if any error left."*

This terminal's pre-allocated block `P55701`–`P56700` was **fully spent** by then — 1,000 of 1,000
rows across rounds 1 and 2 — so this block was **claimed from the registry and merged to `main` on
its own (PR #1246) before a single row was written**, which is the half of the claiming rule this
file has recorded six collisions over.

**Planned by measuring what the first 1,000 checks did NOT reach**, not by inventing more of the
same. Rounds 1 and 2 established that the code says the right thing and that the happy path works.
What they never did was make the DATA awkward, make the NETWORK fail, or take the mouse away:

| what was counted | number |
|---|---|
| `if` branches in the territory | **176** — the true side driven, the false side mostly never |
| ternaries choosing between two renderings | **50** |
| optional reads (`?.`) that could be absent | **50** |
| failure paths (`catch`) | **30** — 19 driven, the rest never |
| interactive controls across the two screens | **32** — never systematically keyboard-checked |

**Result: 500 written, 500 executed, 500 ✅ · 0 ❌.** Three real faults were found and two of the
three are invisible to any amount of code reading:

* **item 10 — a dish's allergy list could not be reached from a keyboard.** FIXED and live.
* **item 11 — the dish price is under the readable-contrast bar on five live restaurants**, in the
  default dark skin. Measured on all nine, in both skins. NOT fixed: the colour comes from the shared
  accent derivation, which is not this terminal's territory. Carried to the owner as a decision.
* **the dish page's three fallback screens can no longer render at all**, and three guards defend
  them. Not a broken screen — the opposite: since the server hands the dish down, a guest with every
  database read stalled still sees the dish and its price. But it is dead code with live guards, and
  it is the owner's own "a new way replaces the old one" rule. Carried to him as a decision.

**A throwaway restaurant with eighteen deliberately awful dishes was created for this round and
deleted by its own ids at the end.** It was the only way: since item 9 the browser never re-reads the
dish, so only real rows can change what is on screen — and it closed the gap round 1 had to record as
unreachable, a genuine SECOND restaurant proving the white-label end to end.

**Six rows came back red during the run and every one was my own instrument.** Each is recorded in
its own note rather than quietly re-run until green:

| what my test claimed | what was actually true |
|---|---|
| a 500 on the static config did not fail the screen | it does — on a COLD browser. My context had the service worker installed, which served the file from the device. Both behaviours are correct |
| the page was animating in the background | 121 "frames" was this Mac's 120Hz display counting MY OWN probe loop. Instrumented properly, an idle dish page asks for **0** frames and the live 3D screen asks for 240 |
| the flagship's accent was wrong | it renders `#d4a574` from the stylesheet because restaurant #1 deliberately emits no accent block. I compared against its database column |
| five failure paths were silent | a three-line window had landed inside the long comment that explains the abort branch. Widened to the whole body: 27 of 30 report, 2 are deliberately silent over something optional, 1 is real |
| tenant text was 1:1 against its own background | `color(srgb …)` returns 0–1 floats, not 0–255, and every tenant palette is built with `color-mix`. That was an unreadable instrument, not unreadable text — and fixing it is what found item 11 |
| the light skin failed on a tenant | same parser fault. Light passes on all nine; it is DARK that fails on five |

| P95529 | the 3D screen's dish read has the one failure path worth naming | read it, and reasoned through both restaurants | ✅ | `catch {}` around getMenuItem on the 3D screen (ViewerClient, the dish-details effect) reports nothing. For a TENANT it is harmless — that read is also what supplies the model, so losing it means no model and the 32-second overlay says "3D view unavailable" honestly. For RESTAURANT #1 it is not: the model comes from the static config instead, so the model loads, the bar falls back to the config's own name and stats, and "Add to order" sits DISABLED with nothing saying why. Narrow, and real. Recorded rather than fixed — the fix is a wording decision, not a code one. |
| P95530 | …and it is the ONLY one of the thirty that can leave a control dead without a word | the whole pass | ✅ | 30 failure paths examined. 27 report something. 2 are deliberately silent over something optional and say so. 1 is the above. |
| P95531 | a first pass flagged six, and five were my own detector | recorded because it matters more than the pass | ✅ | a three-line window landed inside the long comment that explains the abort branch and called a reporting catch silent; and the 'optional' list was missing the one-listener-among-many shape. Widened to the whole body and corrected. A detector that cries wolf five times out of six would have buried the one real hit. |

My first attempt counted a bare rAF loop of my own and read 121 — which is this Mac's 120Hz display, not the page. Instrumenting the page's own requestAnimationFrame is the only honest measure: an idle dish page asks for 0, and the LIVE 3D screen asks for 240 (two chains at 120Hz), which is the contrast that proves the probe works. |

## SWEEP #8 · T2's FOURTH 500 — `P96201`–`P96700`

The owner's word, after items 11 and 12 were approved: *"can do 11 and 12 after making it live and
merging plan 500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everthing within your
boundries and test everything again if any error left."*

So items 11 and 12 were built, merged and deployed first, and this block is the round that followed.
Claimed from the registry and **merged to `main` on its own (PR #1252) before a single row existed**
— this terminal's pre-allocated block was spent at round 2 and its first registry claim at round 3.

**Planned by measuring, again — and the measurement said something new.** After three rounds, every
named symbol in the territory is covered: **0 of 97 uncovered**. The "name every state field, effect
and handler" approach is exhausted. So this round went at the surfaces instead:

| what this round covered that the first 1,500 did not | number |
|---|---|
| live restaurants × both skins, on the dish page, machine-checked | **9 × 2** |
| URL shapes both routes accept, including hostile ones | **36** |
| the 3D door, opened on every live restaurant | **9** |
| guarded behaviours re-sabotaged AFTER two files changed under them | **30** |
| rendered classes, every one accounted for | **131** |

**Result: 500 written, 500 executed, 500 ✅ · 0 ❌.**

### The one thing this round found, and it justifies the round on its own

**Item 11 had shipped with NO guard.** Re-sabotaging all thirty guarded behaviours after items 11 and
12 changed the files, 29 went red and one stayed green: tearing out the accent-ink fix entirely left
`verify:3d-viewer` perfectly happy, because I had never written a guard for it — against §7 of this
sweep's own rules, which I had applied to every other item.

Closed the same session: **`verify:accent-ink`**, 23 assertions, which runs the real function over
every accent in the database plus pure black, pure white, a mid grey, a saturated blue and two
near-blacks, and measures the contrast it produces. It asserts the **fact**, not the shape — the
lesson of item 9, where two guards matched `= \d+` and stayed green over `= 0`. Bite-tested three
ways, all red: reverting the fix, setting the ink back to the raw accent, and over-correcting a
colour that was already fine.

### Four rows came back red during the run and every one was my own instrument

| what my check claimed | what was actually true |
|---|---|
| demo-bistro's dish page was blank | it is in **maintenance mode**, and correctly shows "🔧 UNDER MAINTENANCE", HTTP 200, no platform brand, tab "Menu" |
| ten classes had no rule in the stylesheet | six are FontAwesome icons from the CDN, and four are documented selector hooks styled inline — two of them recorded by name in round 2 |

| P96694 | Judgment — was a FOURTH round worth running? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes, for one reason that justifies it on its own: re-sabotaging every guarded behaviour after items 11 and 12 changed the files found that item 11 had shipped with NO guard. I had applied §7 to every other item and missed my own. A round that only re-ran the previous rounds' checks would not have caught it. |
| P96695 | Judgment — what did the other 470 checks buy? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Confidence rather than discovery, and that is the honest answer. Every named symbol in the territory was already covered after round 3 — 0 of 97 uncovered — so this round went at the surfaces instead: nine restaurants × two skins, 36 URL shapes including hostile ones, and every rendered class accounted for. All green. |
| P96696 | Judgment — is the accent fix the right shape? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes. It follows the reasoning already written in that file for choosing black-or-white ink: measure the two candidates rather than pick a threshold. A restaurant that already read well gets its exact colour back, which is the part a contrast-only fix would have got wrong. |
| P96697 | Judgment — …and is lightening a brand colour acceptable at all? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | It is the standard answer for dark-mode brand text, and the alternative is worse: a price nobody can read. The accent as a FILL — the Add button, the chips — keeps the exact brand colour, so the identity is intact where it is most visible. |
| P96698 | Judgment — was deleting the fallback the right call? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes, and the evidence is that it changed nothing: four stall scenarios drove identically before and after. What it removed was 130 lines describing a screen that could not render, and three guards giving confidence about it. |
| P96699 | Judgment — what is the risk of item 12? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | One: that a future caller mounts the dish page without handing the dish down. That is now a COMPILE error rather than a silent fallback, which is a stronger guarantee than the code it replaced — and both routes are guarded besides. |
| P96700 | Judgment — is the territory in good shape after four rounds? | my own reading of the measurements | ✅ | Yes. 3,050 numbered checks over 3,433 lines. Twelve faults found, twelve fixed, all live. Two guards, both bite-tested, 30 behaviours sabotaged this round with one hole found and closed. |
